Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n98\t    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n99\t    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n100\t    parallel\" pattern.\n101\t\n102\t    Parameters\n103\t    ----------\n104\t    command:\n105\t        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n106\t        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n107\t    env:\n108\t        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n109\t        environment. It deliberately avoids any in-process\nSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\nstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n\nThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n  newline handling. Used for control sequences, partial-line input, or\n  test-stub probing.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.read_stdout` -- non-blocking read of the next chunk of bytes\n  from the PTY. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n  exit code. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n            return self._exit_code\n\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; emulate one by polling\n        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n        try:\n            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n\n        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n        try:\n            child.wait()\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n            pass\n\n        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n        else:\n            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ cleanup\n\n    def terminate(self, force: bool = False) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send SIGTERM (or SIGKILL when ``force=True``) to the child.\n\n        Safe to call when the child has already exited.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None or not self._child.isalive():\n            return\n        self._child.terminate(force=force)\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Close the PTY file descriptors and release pexpect's bookkeeping.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._child.close(force=False)\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # close() can raise when the child is still alive; force-close to\n            # avoid leaking the PTY fd. terminate() is safe to call twice.\n            try:\n                self._child.close(force=True)\n            except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n                pass\n        if self._child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(self._child.signalstatus)\n        elif self._child.exitstatus is not None and self._exit_code is None:\n            self._exit_code = int(self._child.exitstatus)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    @property\n    def exit_code(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"Last-captured exit code (``None`` until the child has exited).\"\"\"\n\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    def fileno(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the PTY's read file descriptor for ``select``-based loops.\"\"\"\n\n        return int(self._require_child().fileno())\n","numLines":426,"startLine":1,"totalLines":426}}}

This makes it safe to\n26\tshare across threads inside the parallel orchestrator (R-058 / T03.16)\n27\twithout locking. Idempotency is one-\n62\t  shot per instance: calling :meth:`setup` twice raises ``RuntimeError``\n63\t  rather than silently leaking a directory.\n64\t* :meth:`env`                   — returns the env-var dict the subprocess\n65\t  needs: ``HOME`` always, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always, and\n66\t  ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when ``time_offset_sec != 0``. The contents are split for grep-ability:\n131\t\n132\t    * Line 1 is the exception class name verbatim\n133\t      (``type(exc).__name__``), so a downstream EvalRunner can bucket\n134\t      the error with a single ``head -n1`` read.\n135\t    * The remainder is the formatted traceback for forensic inspection.\n136\t\n137\t    The helper is best-effort: ``setup`` catches and discards any\n138\t    exception this function raises so a write failure (e.g. the\n139\t    ``.eval-meta`` directory cannot be created because the per-eval HOME\n140\t    was made read-only by a future hardening step) never masks the\n141\t    original setup exception.\n142\t\n143\t    Args:\n144\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n145\t            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. The guard refuses to\n282\t            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n283\t            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. The\n290\t            ``check`` attribute identifies which one; ``detail`` carries\n291\t            the human-readable cause; ``__cause__`` holds the underlying\n292\t            exception when applicable.\n293\t    \"\"\"\n294\t\n295\t    # Check 1: eval_id regex. The dataclass\n367\t      stores it as-is; full containment is FR-ISO2's responsibility\n368\t      (T02.08).\n369\t    * ``session_id``     — ``str``, required. type: ignore[attr-defined]\n435\t\n436\t    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n437\t        \"\"\"Create the per-eval HOME directory under ``home_root``.\n438\t\n439\t        Uses :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` with the eval_id as prefix so\n440\t        sibling per-eval HOMEs under the same ``home_root`` are\n441\t        guaranteed to be unique by the OS (atomic on POSIX, see\n442\t        ``mkdtemp`` docs). Parallel evals never collide on directory\n443\t        names; mutating one HOME cannot affect siblings because each\n444\t        HOME is its own ``mkdtemp`` root.\n445\t\n446\t        After ``mkdtemp`` succeeds, :func:`containment_guard` (FR-ISO2,\n447\t        T02.08) is applied AFTER the per-eval HOME has been\n448\t        materialized and BEFORE any hook deploy or eval-state write.\n449\t        The guard re-validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 regex,\n450\t        re-applies the AC12 scratch-root allowlist, and\n451\t        ``Path.resolve(strict=True)``-checks the freshly created HOME\n452\t        against the scratch root. Any failure surfaces as\n453\t        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`; the partial per-eval HOME is\n454\t        left on disk so callers can route teardown through\n455\t        ``teardown(keep=True)`` for forensic inspection (the\n456\t        containment exception itself carries the full forensic\n457\t        payload — see NFR-ISO2 contract below).\n458\t\n459\t        Idempotency rule: calling :meth:`setup` twice on the same\n460\t        instance raises :class:`RuntimeError`. This prevents the silent\n461\t        directory leak that would otherwise occur if a caller looped\n462\t        over an instance, and it makes the atomic-setup wrapper\n463\t        (T02.13) easier to reason about.\n464\t\n465\t        Args:\n466\t            config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n467\t                ``allowed_scratch_roots`` provides the scratch-root\n468\t                allowlist :func:`containment_guard` enforces.\n469\t                ``setup`` deliberately refuses to synthesize a\n470\t                fallback that injects ``self.home_root`` into the\n471\t                allowlist — that would defeat the AC12 check, because a\n472\t                caller can construct ``HomeIsolation`` with any\n473\t                ``home_root`` (per-instance, the dataclass cannot\n474\t                meaningfully validate it). Production callers\n475\t                (orchestrator T03.16) pass the run's resolved config;\n476\t                tests build an :class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist\n477\t                includes the test scratch root.\n478\t\n479\t        Returns:\n480\t            The freshly created per-eval HOME path (also recorded on\n481\t            :attr:`home_path`).\n482\t\n483\t        Raises:\n484\t            RuntimeError: If :meth:`setup` was already called on this\n485\t                instance and :meth:`teardown` has not run since.\n486\t            HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three FR-ISO2 checks\n487\t                failed; the per-eval HOME directory was created and is\n488\t                preserved on disk for inspection with a\n489\t                ``setup_failed`` tag (NFR-ISO2 — see below).\n490\t\n491\t        NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract (T02.13 / D-0033)\n492\t        ------------------------------------------------\n493\t\n494\t        Any exception raised AFTER :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` and BEFORE\n495\t        ``setup`` returns successfully MUST leave the per-eval HOME on\n496\t        disk. The private ``_home_path`` slot stays populated so a\n497\t        caller that wraps ``setup`` in ``try/finally`` can route\n498\t        teardown through ``teardown(keep=True)`` to preserve the\n499\t        partial HOME for forensics.\n500\t\n501\t        Non-containment exceptions additionally drop a ``setup_failed``\n502\t        artifact tag at ``<home>/.eval-meta/setup_failed`` (constant\n503\t        :data:`SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH`). The tag's first line is the\n504\t        exception class name; the body is the formatted traceback. The\n505\t        tag distinguishes harness bugs / environmental refusal (status\n506\t        ERRORED — tag present, no eval output) from real eval failures\n507\t        (status FAIL — eval ran, expectations missed). The tag write\n508\t        is best-effort: a secondary exception during tag write is\n509\t        discarded so the original exception is the one the caller\n510\t        sees. The original exception type is preserved verbatim — the\n511\t        wrapper adds the tag side effect, it does not re-wrap or\n512\t        re-classify the exception.\n513\t\n514\t        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` is the deliberate exception\n515\t        to the tag-write rule: writing under a HOME the guard just\n516\t        refused could land under the real ``~/.claude/`` directory\n517\t        when the scratch root or the ``mkdtemp`` result is symlinked\n518\t        there (the attack matrix exercised by NFR-SEC3 in T02.10).\n519\t        The violation exception itself is the forensic signal — its\n520\t        ``check``, ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id`` and\n521\t        ``detail`` attributes carry everything a reporter needs to\n522\t        bucket the eval as ERRORED.\n523\t        \"\"\"\n524\t\n525\t        if self._home_path is not None:  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n526\t            raise RuntimeError(\n527\t                f\"HomeIsolation.setup() already called for eval_id={self.eval_id!r}\"\n528\t            )\n529\t\n530\t        # Ensure the scratch root exists. ``parents=True, exist_ok=True``\n531\t        # is safe because the FR-ISO2 guard below catches any path that\n532\t        # resolves outside the policy allowlist.\n533\t        self.home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n534\t\n535\t        home = Path(\n536\t            tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))\n537\t        )\n538\t        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", home)\n539\t\n540\t        # NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper. The ``_home_path`` slot stays populated for the\n546\t        # same reason.\n547\t        #\n548\t        # Tag-write asymmetry between containment and non-containment\n549\t        # exceptions:\n550\t        #\n551\t        # * :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` — NO tag is written.\n552\t        #   Writing a file under a HOME the guard just refused would\n553\t        #   breach the NFR-SEC3 invariants enforced in T02.10\n554\t        #   (`test_hard_guard_real_home.py`): when ``scratch_root`` is\n555\t        #   (or symlinks to) the real ``~/.claude/``, or when\n556\t        #   :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` returned a symlinked path that\n557\t        #   resolves outside ``scratch_root``, any write under ``home``\n558\t        #   lands inside real ``~/.claude/``. The\n583\t            # containment exception itself is the structured signal.\n584\t            raise\n585\t        except Exception as exc:\n586\t            try:\n587\t                _write_setup_failed_tag(home, exc)\n588\t            except Exception:\n589\t                pass\n590\t            raise\n591\t        return home\n592\t\n593\t    def env(self) -> dict[str, str]:\n594\t        \"\"\"Return the env-var dict the subprocess needs for HOME isolation.\n595\t\n596\t        * ``HOME`` is always present.\n597\t        * ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is always present (FR-ISO1 mandate).\n598\t        * ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` is present only when\n599\t          ``time_offset_sec`` differs from the default ``0``. The COMP-012 probe at\n``tests/cli/eval/test_isolation_layers_probe.py`` (Task T02.05) must keep\npassing after this extension, which it does because nothing in\n``cli/sprint/executor.py`` is touched.\n\nDM-006 record (T02.04) — the four immutable fields\n====================================================\n\n``HomeIsolation`` is a ``@dataclass(frozen=True)`` so once an instance is\nbuilt it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers. This makes it safe to\nshare across threads inside the parallel orchestrator (R-058 / T03.16)\nwithout locking. Equality is structural across all four fields via the\ndataclass-generated ``__eq__``.\n\n1. ``eval_id``         — the FR-SCH2-validated eval identifier. The id\n   flows directly into the per-eval HOME by :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`\n   so it MUST satisfy the FR-SCH2 regex before any filesystem write. The\n   ``__post_init__`` hook re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05) so\n   a ``HomeIsolation`` can never be constructed with an unsafe id even\n   when a caller bypasses the loader.\n2. ``home_root``       — the scratch root directory under which the\n   per-eval HOME is ``mkdtemp``-ed by :meth:`setup`. Full FR-ISO2 path\n   containment lands in T02.08; this module performs only the minimal\n   ``parents=True, exist_ok=True`` materialization needed to create\n   sibling per-eval HOMEs concurrently.\n3. ``session_id``      — the value :meth:`env` stamps into\n   ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID``. Allocation lives in the orchestrator (FR-G2 /\n   T03.16); this record only holds the assigned value.\n4. ``time_offset_sec`` — optional offset in whole seconds for the eval's\n   simulated wall clock. Default ``0`` matches DM-006 verbatim; when the\n   value is non-zero :meth:`env` adds ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET``. Whether\n   the subprocess actually honors the variable is gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8\n   / T06.03); keeping the offset opt-in via non-zero values means the\n   env dict stays minimal until OQ-8 resolves.\n\nCOMP-006 method surface (T02.07) — FR-ISO1\n==========================================\n\nThe four methods below are the externally observable surface of\nCOMP-006. They mutate process-local filesystem state (and an internal\nprivate slot tracking the created HOME path) without ever mutating the\nfour DM-006 fields:\n\n* :meth:`setup`                 — ``mkdtemp`` under ``home_root`` and stash\n  the created path on a private ``_home_path`` slot via\n  ``object.__setattr__``. Returns the created path. Idempotency is one-\n  shot per instance: calling :meth:`setup` twice raises ``RuntimeError``\n  rather than silently leaking a directory.\n* :meth:`env`                   — returns the env-var dict the subprocess\n  needs: ``HOME`` always, ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` always, and\n  ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when ``time_offset_sec != 0``. The\n  return type pins ``dict[str, str]`` so callers can ``.update()`` it\n  alongside ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` without coercion.\n* :meth:`teardown`              — removes the per-eval HOME when ``keep``\n  is false, leaves it on disk when ``keep`` is true (so post-mortem\n  inspection or NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` tagging in T02.13 can still\n  pick the directory up). Always clears the private path slot so the\n  instance is safe to garbage-collect.\n* :meth:`state_path`            — joins ``suffix`` under the active\n  per-eval HOME so consumers (hook adapter T02.14, atomic setup T02.13,\n  artifact writers) never need to import :mod:`os.path` themselves. The\n  full containment check on ``suffix`` lands in T02.08; this method\n  performs the minimum necessary check that the joined path stays under\n  ``home_path``.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* DM-006 record spec (roadmap row 26).\n* COMP-006 method surface (roadmap rows 28 / 32; T02.07 / T02.11).\n* FR-SCH2 :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n* FR-ISO2 ``containment_guard`` (T02.08) — strengthens the symlink /\n  allowlist / regex checks layered on top of :meth:`setup` and\n  :meth:`state_path`.\n* DOC-OQ8 (T06.03) — gates ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` semantics.\n* COMP-012 probe (T02.05 — ``test_isolation_layers_probe.py``) — pins the\n  upstream ``IsolationLayers`` shape this module sits beside.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nimport tempfile\nimport traceback\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom .config import EvalConfig, ScratchRootViolation, resolve_scratch_root\nfrom .loader import InvalidEvalId, validate_eval_id\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\",\n    \"HomeContainmentViolation\",\n    \"HomeIsolation\",\n    \"containment_guard\",\n]\n\n\n# NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract — see ``HomeIsolation.setup`` docstring and\n# D-0033/spec.md. The tag is written under the per-eval HOME so that the\n# directory becomes self-describing for downstream consumers (the orchestrator,\n# the EvalRunner, post-mortem operators): a HOME that carries this relative\n# path is one whose setup raised AFTER ``tempfile.mkdtemp`` materialized the\n# directory and BEFORE ``setup`` returned successfully. The constant is\n# exported so :class:`EvalRunner` (T03.x) and forensics tooling can locate the\n# tag without re-deriving the path.\nSETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH = \".eval-meta/setup_failed\"\n\n\ndef _write_setup_failed_tag(home_path: Path, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Write the NFR-ISO2 ``setup_failed`` artifact tag under ``home_path``.\n\n    The tag distinguishes \"harness bug or environmental refusal during\n    setup\" (status ERRORED) from \"eval ran and produced a failing\n    assertion\" (status FAIL). The contents are split for grep-ability:\n\n    * Line 1 is the exception class name verbatim\n      (``type(exc).__name__``), so a downstream EvalRunner can bucket\n      the error with a single ``head -n1`` read.\n    * The remainder is the formatted traceback for forensic inspection.\n\n    The helper is best-effort: ``setup`` catches and discards any\n    exception this function raises so a write failure (e.g. the\n    ``.eval-meta`` directory cannot be created because the per-eval HOME\n    was made read-only by a future hardening step) never masks the\n    original setup exception.\n\n    Args:\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. The tag is materialized at\n            ``home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH``.\n        exc: The exception that interrupted ``setup``. Used only to\n            populate the tag contents; this helper never re-raises it.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    tag_path = home_path / SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH\n    tag_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    # ``traceback.format_exception`` reads the active exception state when\n    # the helper is invoked from inside an ``except`` clause. Pin the\n    # arguments explicitly so the helper also works when called outside\n    # one (defensive — the only call site is currently inside ``except``).\n    tb_lines = traceback.format_exception(type(exc), exc, exc.__traceback__)\n    payload = f\"{type(exc).__name__}\\n\" + \"\".join(tb_lines)\n    tag_path.write_text(payload, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n\n\nclass HomeContainmentViolation(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`containment_guard` when any FR-ISO2 check fails.\n\n    The exception is the single hard-failure surface of FR-ISO2 — it is\n    the only thing :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` may raise once\n    :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` has succeeded and BEFORE the hook adapter\n    (T02.14) writes a single byte under the per-eval HOME. Catching this\n    type at the orchestrator level is sufficient to cover all three\n    containment checks (eval_id regex, scratch-root allowlist, post-\n    mkdtemp symlink resolution); callers MUST NOT branch on the\n    underlying :class:`InvalidEvalId` / :class:`ScratchRootViolation`\n    causes — they are exposed via ``__cause__`` for forensics only.\n\n    The exception MUST raise before any hook deploy or eval-state\n    write so NFR-SEC2 (\"hard refusal before side effects\") is upheld;\n    the NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is responsible for\n    preserving the partial per-eval HOME with a ``setup_failed`` tag\n    once it lands.\n\n    Attributes:\n        check: Short identifier for the failed check (``\"eval_id\"``,\n            ``\"scratch_root_allowlist\"``, ``\"home_path_resolution\"`` or\n            ``\"home_path_escape\"``). Lets reporters bucket failures\n            without parsing the human-readable message.\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME path the guard was asked to\n            validate. Stored verbatim as supplied by the caller (no\n            resolution) so forensics keep the original input.\n        scratch_root: The declared scratch root the guard checked\n            ``home_path`` against. Stored verbatim.\n        eval_id: The eval id under inspection. Stored verbatim\n            (``repr``-rendered) so non-string smuggled scalars are\n            visible in reporter output.\n        detail: Free-form one-line message describing the specific\n            failure. Inherits from the underlying cause when one\n            exists (e.g. the ``ScratchRootViolation`` rendering).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        check: str,\n        home_path: Path,\n        scratch_root: Path,\n        eval_id: object,\n        detail: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.check = check\n        self.home_path = home_path\n        self.scratch_root = scratch_root\n        self.eval_id = eval_id\n        self.detail = detail\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"FR-ISO2 containment check {check!r} failed for \"\n            f\"eval_id={eval_id!r} home_path={home_path!s} \"\n            f\"scratch_root={scratch_root!s}: {detail}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef containment_guard(\n    home_path: Path,\n    scratch_root: Path,\n    eval_id: str,\n    *,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"FR-ISO2 three-check defense-in-depth guard.\n\n    The guard layers three independent checks on top of the per-eval\n    HOME materialization that :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` just performed.\n    Each check has a distinct attack vector in scope:\n\n    1. **eval_id regex** — re-applies :func:`validate_eval_id` (T01.05).\n       The loader (T01.07) already validates the id pre-expand and\n       post-expand, but a caller bypassing :class:`SuiteLoader` (e.g.,\n       a programmatic test, a future REPL entry point) could construct\n       a :class:`HomeIsolation` directly. The re-check closes that\n       loader-bypass surface called out in NFR-SEC2.\n    2. **Scratch-root allowlist** — re-applies\n       :func:`resolve_scratch_root` (T01.19) against ``scratch_root``.\n       The helper resolves the path with ``Path.resolve(strict=False)``\n       which collapses symlinks; if ``scratch_root`` is a symlink to a\n       non-allowlisted target (e.g., ``/home/user/.claude``) the\n       resolved form will not match any entry in the resolved\n       :attr:`EvalConfig.allowed_scratch_roots`. The allowlist is\n       sourced exclusively from the caller-supplied\n       :class:`EvalConfig`; nothing inside this module synthesizes a\n       fallback that could inject the caller's ``scratch_root``\n       into the allowlist after the fact (NFR-SEC2 loader-bypass\n       defense — see D-0029 spec rationale).\n    3. **Post-mkdtemp path containment** — resolves ``home_path`` with\n       ``Path.resolve(strict=True)`` and asserts the resolved form is\n       equal to OR a sub-path of the resolved ``scratch_root``. The\n       ``strict=True`` mode raises :class:`FileNotFoundError` if the\n       per-eval HOME has not been materialized yet, so this is the\n       check that pins \"guard runs AFTER ``mkdtemp``\". Catches the\n       attack vector where a symlinked component inside ``home_path``\n       points outside ``scratch_root`` even though the textual prefix\n       check would have passed.\n\n    On any failure the function raises :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`\n    with the failing check identifier. Underlying causes\n    (:class:`InvalidEvalId`, :class:`ScratchRootViolation`,\n    :class:`FileNotFoundError`) are chained via ``raise ... from`` so\n    forensic tooling can still walk ``__cause__``.\n\n    Args:\n        home_path: Path to the per-eval HOME directory ``mkdtemp``-ed by\n            :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Must exist on disk; the\n            ``strict=True`` resolve enforces this so a caller cannot\n            invoke the guard \"ahead\" of ``mkdtemp``.\n        scratch_root: Scratch root under which ``home_path`` must\n            reside. Need not exist (``strict=False`` resolution); for\n            test ergonomics callers can pass a path that\n            :class:`HomeIsolation` will create via\n            ``mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)`` shortly after.\n        eval_id: The eval identifier the per-eval HOME corresponds to.\n            Re-validated through :func:`validate_eval_id`.\n        config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n            ``allowed_scratch_roots`` is the sole source of truth for\n            the scratch-root allowlist check. The guard refuses to\n            synthesize a fallback config: a missing argument is a\n            programming error, never a \"use the defaults\" cue. This\n            closes the bypass where a caller could otherwise construct\n            ``HomeIsolation(home_root=<attacker-controlled>)`` and have\n            the guard quietly add that root to the allowlist.\n\n    Raises:\n        HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three checks failed. The\n            ``check`` attribute identifies which one; ``detail`` carries\n            the human-readable cause; ``__cause__`` holds the underlying\n            exception when applicable.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Check 1: eval_id regex. Loader-bypass defense.\n    try:\n        validate_eval_id(eval_id)\n    except InvalidEvalId as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"eval_id\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=str(exc),\n        ) from exc\n\n    # Check 2: scratch-root allowlist (symlink-aware via resolve_scratch_root).\n    # ``config`` is required: see docstring rationale (NFR-SEC2 bypass close).\n    try:\n        resolved_scratch = resolve_scratch_root(scratch_root, config=config)\n    except ScratchRootViolation as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"scratch_root_allowlist\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=str(exc),\n        ) from exc\n\n    # Check 3: post-mkdtemp symlink resolution + containment under\n    # scratch_root. strict=True asserts the HOME exists (i.e., mkdtemp\n    # already ran). Any symlink inside home_path is collapsed; the\n    # resolved form must equal or live beneath the resolved scratch root.\n    try:\n        resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=True)\n    except FileNotFoundError as exc:\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"home_path_resolution\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=(\n                \"home_path does not exist on disk; FR-ISO2 requires the \"\n                \"guard to run AFTER mkdtemp materialized the per-eval \"\n                \"HOME\"\n            ),\n        ) from exc\n\n    if not (\n        resolved_home == resolved_scratch\n        or resolved_home.is_relative_to(resolved_scratch)\n    ):\n        raise HomeContainmentViolation(\n            check=\"home_path_escape\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            scratch_root=scratch_root,\n            eval_id=eval_id,\n            detail=(\n                f\"resolved home_path {resolved_home!s} escapes resolved \"\n                f\"scratch_root {resolved_scratch!s}\"\n            ),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass HomeIsolation:\n    \"\"\"Per-eval HOME isolation record + COMP-006 method surface.\n\n    DM-006 fixes the immutable four-field record contract:\n\n    * ``eval_id``        — ``str``, required. Re-validated in\n      ``__post_init__`` via :func:`validate_eval_id` so any unsafe id\n      raises :class:`superclaude.cli.eval.InvalidEvalId` BEFORE any\n      consumer can derive a filesystem path from it.\n    * ``home_root``      — ``pathlib.Path``, required. Scratch root under\n      which the per-eval HOME is created by :meth:`setup`. The dataclass\n      stores it as-is; full containment is FR-ISO2's responsibility\n      (T02.08).\n    * ``session_id``     — ``str``, required. Value :meth:`env` stamps\n      into ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` for the subprocess. Allocation lives in\n      the orchestrator (FR-G2 / T03.16); this record only holds the\n      assigned value.\n    * ``time_offset_sec`` — ``int``, defaults to ``0``. Optional simulated\n      wall-clock offset in whole seconds; :meth:`env` emits\n      ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` only when non-zero. Full activation is\n      gated on OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8 / T06.03).\n\n    The four declared fields are frozen — mutation raises\n    ``dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError``. A single private slot,\n    ``_home_path``, tracks the post-:meth:`setup` HOME directory; it is\n    written via ``object.__setattr__`` (the documented escape hatch for\n    frozen dataclasses) and never participates in equality or hashing.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    home_root: Path\n    session_id: str\n    time_offset_sec: int = 0\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Re-apply the FR-SCH2 guard and initialize the private path slot.\n\n        The FR-SCH2 re-validation makes loader-bypass impossible:\n        constructing a ``HomeIsolation`` with an unsafe ``eval_id``\n        raises :class:`InvalidEvalId` before any filesystem operation\n        becomes reachable. The private ``_home_path`` slot is set to\n        ``None`` so :meth:`env` / :meth:`state_path` can raise a clear\n        error when called before :meth:`setup`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        validate_eval_id(self.eval_id)\n        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", None)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # COMP-006 method surface (FR-ISO1)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @property\n    def home_path(self) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Return the per-eval HOME directory created by :meth:`setup`.\n\n        Raises :class:`RuntimeError` when :meth:`setup` has not yet been\n        called or when :meth:`teardown` has already cleared the slot,\n        so the failure mode is \"obvious AttributeError\" instead of a\n        silent ``None`` flowing into an env dict.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        home = self._home_path  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n        if home is None:\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                \"HomeIsolation.setup() must be called before accessing home_path\"\n            )\n        return home\n\n    @property\n    def is_set_up(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` when :meth:`setup` has created a per-eval HOME\n        and :meth:`teardown` has not yet been called.\n\n        Lets callers (atomic setup wrapper T02.13, the orchestrator)\n        ask whether teardown is needed without catching exceptions.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return self._home_path is not None  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n\n    def setup(self, *, config: EvalConfig) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Create the per-eval HOME directory under ``home_root``.\n\n        Uses :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` with the eval_id as prefix so\n        sibling per-eval HOMEs under the same ``home_root`` are\n        guaranteed to be unique by the OS (atomic on POSIX, see\n        ``mkdtemp`` docs). Parallel evals never collide on directory\n        names; mutating one HOME cannot affect siblings because each\n        HOME is its own ``mkdtemp`` root.\n\n        After ``mkdtemp`` succeeds, :func:`containment_guard` (FR-ISO2,\n        T02.08) is applied AFTER the per-eval HOME has been\n        materialized and BEFORE any hook deploy or eval-state write.\n        The guard re-validates ``eval_id`` against the FR-SCH2 regex,\n        re-applies the AC12 scratch-root allowlist, and\n        ``Path.resolve(strict=True)``-checks the freshly created HOME\n        against the scratch root. Any failure surfaces as\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation`; the partial per-eval HOME is\n        left on disk so callers can route teardown through\n        ``teardown(keep=True)`` for forensic inspection (the\n        containment exception itself carries the full forensic\n        payload — see NFR-ISO2 contract below).\n\n        Idempotency rule: calling :meth:`setup` twice on the same\n        instance raises :class:`RuntimeError`. This prevents the silent\n        directory leak that would otherwise occur if a caller looped\n        over an instance, and it makes the atomic-setup wrapper\n        (T02.13) easier to reason about.\n\n        Args:\n            config: Required :class:`EvalConfig`. Its\n                ``allowed_scratch_roots`` provides the scratch-root\n                allowlist :func:`containment_guard` enforces.\n                ``setup`` deliberately refuses to synthesize a\n                fallback that injects ``self.home_root`` into the\n                allowlist — that would defeat the AC12 check, because a\n                caller can construct ``HomeIsolation`` with any\n                ``home_root`` (per-instance, the dataclass cannot\n                meaningfully validate it). Production callers\n                (orchestrator T03.16) pass the run's resolved config;\n                tests build an :class:`EvalConfig` whose allowlist\n                includes the test scratch root.\n\n        Returns:\n            The freshly created per-eval HOME path (also recorded on\n            :attr:`home_path`).\n\n        Raises:\n            RuntimeError: If :meth:`setup` was already called on this\n                instance and :meth:`teardown` has not run since.\n            HomeContainmentViolation: Any of the three FR-ISO2 checks\n                failed; the per-eval HOME directory was created and is\n                preserved on disk for inspection with a\n                ``setup_failed`` tag (NFR-ISO2 — see below).\n\n        NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup contract (T02.13 / D-0033)\n        ------------------------------------------------\n\n        Any exception raised AFTER :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` and BEFORE\n        ``setup`` returns successfully MUST leave the per-eval HOME on\n        disk. The private ``_home_path`` slot stays populated so a\n        caller that wraps ``setup`` in ``try/finally`` can route\n        teardown through ``teardown(keep=True)`` to preserve the\n        partial HOME for forensics.\n\n        Non-containment exceptions additionally drop a ``setup_failed``\n        artifact tag at ``<home>/.eval-meta/setup_failed`` (constant\n        :data:`SETUP_FAILED_TAG_RELPATH`). The tag's first line is the\n        exception class name; the body is the formatted traceback. The\n        tag distinguishes harness bugs / environmental refusal (status\n        ERRORED — tag present, no eval output) from real eval failures\n        (status FAIL — eval ran, expectations missed). The tag write\n        is best-effort: a secondary exception during tag write is\n        discarded so the original exception is the one the caller\n        sees. The original exception type is preserved verbatim — the\n        wrapper adds the tag side effect, it does not re-wrap or\n        re-classify the exception.\n\n        :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` is the deliberate exception\n        to the tag-write rule: writing under a HOME the guard just\n        refused could land under the real ``~/.claude/`` directory\n        when the scratch root or the ``mkdtemp`` result is symlinked\n        there (the attack matrix exercised by NFR-SEC3 in T02.10).\n        The violation exception itself is the forensic signal — its\n        ``check``, ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id`` and\n        ``detail`` attributes carry everything a reporter needs to\n        bucket the eval as ERRORED.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._home_path is not None:  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n            raise RuntimeError(\n                f\"HomeIsolation.setup() already called for eval_id={self.eval_id!r}\"\n            )\n\n        # Ensure the scratch root exists. ``parents=True, exist_ok=True``\n        # is safe because the FR-ISO2 guard below catches any path that\n        # resolves outside the policy allowlist.\n        self.home_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n        home = Path(\n            tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix=f\"{self.eval_id}-\", dir=str(self.home_root))\n        )\n        object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", home)\n\n        # NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper. Anything that raises beyond this\n        # line — FR-ISO2 containment_guard today, any future post-mkdtemp\n        # work (hook deploy verification, capability stamping) tomorrow —\n        # leaves the per-eval HOME on disk so ``teardown(keep=True)``\n        # preserves the directory for post-mortem inspection without a\n        # re-resolve. The ``_home_path`` slot stays populated for the\n        # same reason.\n        #\n        # Tag-write asymmetry between containment and non-containment\n        # exceptions:\n        #\n        # * :class:`HomeContainmentViolation` — NO tag is written.\n        #   Writing a file under a HOME the guard just refused would\n        #   breach the NFR-SEC3 invariants enforced in T02.10\n        #   (`test_hard_guard_real_home.py`): when ``scratch_root`` is\n        #   (or symlinks to) the real ``~/.claude/``, or when\n        #   :func:`tempfile.mkdtemp` returned a symlinked path that\n        #   resolves outside ``scratch_root``, any write under ``home``\n        #   lands inside real ``~/.claude/``. The containment exception\n        #   itself carries the full forensic payload (``check``,\n        #   ``home_path``, ``scratch_root``, ``eval_id``, ``detail``)\n        #   so the orchestrator can bucket the eval as ERRORED without\n        #   the tag.\n        # * Any other ``Exception`` — tag is written. These are\n        #   harness bugs or post-containment failures (e.g., hook deploy\n        #   verification under T02.14) where the per-eval HOME is known\n        #   to be inside the allowlisted scratch root and the tag is\n        #   safe to write. The tag write is best-effort: a secondary\n        #   failure is swallowed so the original exception surfaces\n        #   unchanged.\n        try:\n            # FR-ISO2 defense-in-depth: re-check eval_id, scratch-root\n            # allowlist, and symlink-resolved containment AFTER mkdtemp\n            # and BEFORE the hook adapter (T02.14) writes anything under\n            # the per-eval HOME.\n            containment_guard(\n                home_path=home,\n                scratch_root=self.home_root,\n                eval_id=self.eval_id,\n                config=config,\n            )\n        except HomeContainmentViolation:\n            # NFR-SEC3: no FS writes under a refused HOME. The\n            # containment exception itself is the structured signal.\n            raise\n        except Exception as exc:\n            try:\n                _write_setup_failed_tag(home, exc)\n            except Exception:\n                pass\n            raise\n        return home\n\n    def env(self) -> dict[str, str]:\n        \"\"\"Return the env-var dict the subprocess needs for HOME isolation.\n\n        * ``HOME`` is always present.\n        * ``CLAUDE_SESSION_ID`` is always present (FR-ISO1 mandate).\n        * ``CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET`` is present only when\n          ``time_offset_sec`` differs from the default ``0``. Keeping the\n          variable opt-in means OQ-8 (DOC-OQ8 / T06.03) does not have to\n          be resolved before this method is safe to call: a caller that\n          does not request a time offset gets the historic two-key dict.\n\n        Callers ``.update()`` this dict alongside\n        ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` to assemble the full subprocess\n        env; the return type is pinned to ``dict[str, str]`` so the\n        merge is type-clean.\n\n        Raises :class:`RuntimeError` if invoked before :meth:`setup`,\n        because the ``HOME`` value would otherwise have to be a\n        fabricated placeholder.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        env: dict[str, str] = {\n            \"HOME\": str(self.home_path),\n            \"CLAUDE_SESSION_ID\": self.session_id,\n        }\n        if self.time_offset_sec != 0:\n            env[\"CLAUDE_FAKE_TIME_OFFSET\"] = str(self.time_offset_sec)\n        return env\n\n    def teardown(self, keep: bool) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Tear down the per-eval HOME directory.\n\n        * ``keep=False`` removes the directory tree (``shutil.rmtree``\n          with ``ignore_errors=False``; any failure surfaces to the\n          caller so the orchestrator can decide whether to flag the\n          eval as cleanup-failed).\n        * ``keep=True`` leaves the directory on disk for post-mortem\n          inspection — this is the branch the NFR-ISO2 atomic setup\n          wrapper (T02.13) takes when ``setup`` partially fails, and the\n          branch :meth:`teardown` is called on under ``--keep-home``\n          (T03.18).\n\n        Either way, the private ``_home_path`` slot is cleared after\n        teardown, so subsequent :meth:`env` / :meth:`state_path` calls\n        fail loudly instead of pointing at a stale (possibly deleted)\n        path. Calling :meth:`teardown` when no HOME has been set up is\n        a no-op — callers are not required to track setup state\n        themselves.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        home = self._home_path  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\n        if home is None:\n            return  # No-op: setup never ran, or teardown already ran.\n\n        try:\n            if not keep:\n                shutil.rmtree(home)\n        finally:\n            # Always clear the slot — even if rmtree raised, the path\n            # is no longer trustworthy for future env() / state_path()\n            # consumers and callers will re-run setup() if they want a\n            # fresh HOME.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"_home_path\", None)\n\n    def state_path(self, suffix: str) -> Path:\n        \"\"\"Return ``home_path / suffix`` after a light containment check.\n\n        ``suffix`` must be a relative path (no leading separator) and\n        must not contain ``..`` components — otherwise the returned\n        path could escape ``home_path`` and undermine the\n        per-eval HOME isolation. Full FR-ISO2 containment\n        (symlink resolution + allowlist) lands in T02.08; this method\n        performs only the minimum check necessary to keep the\n        primitive safe in isolation.\n\n        Raises :class:`ValueError` for any suffix that escapes\n        ``home_path``. Raises :class:`RuntimeError` if invoked before\n        :meth:`setup` (via :attr:`home_path`).\n        \"\"\"\n\n        candidate = Path(suffix)\n        if candidate.is_absolute():\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"state_path suffix must be relative, got absolute path: {suffix!r}\"\n            )\n        if \"..\" in candidate.parts:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"state_path suffix must not contain '..' components: {suffix!r}\"\n            )\n\n        joined = self.home_path / candidate\n        # Defense-in-depth: even with the part check above, refuse any\n        # joined path that does not resolve under home_path. This\n        # complements the FR-ISO2 guard in T02.08 (which will resolve\n        # symlinks); here we work on the lexical path because\n        # state_path may legitimately be called before the suffix\n        # exists on disk.\n        try:\n            joined.relative_to(self.home_path)\n        except ValueError as exc:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"state_path suffix escapes home_path: {suffix!r}\"\n            ) from exc\n        return joined\n","numLines":697,"startLine":1,"totalLines":697}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PSJdhXV7Qg1wWu549NC8UY","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t\"\"\"COMP-014 install_hooks adapter for per-eval HOME (Task T02.14 / D-0034).\n2\t\n3\tThis module is the single integration point between the cliEval harness's\n4\tper-eval ``HOME`` (materialized by :class:`HomeIsolation` in T02.11) and\n5\tthe production hook installer at :mod:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks`.\n6\tThe adapter has two jobs:\n7\t\n8\t1. **Reuse, do not duplicate.** Delegate the script-copy + settings.json\n9\t   merge to the existing ``install_hooks`` so the per-eval hook surface\n10\t   is exactly the production user-install surface. A separate copy\n11\t   pipeline would drift; the COMP-014 reuse contract pins this to the\n12\t   one installer.\n13\t2. **Deploy ``hooks.json`` verbatim.** ``install_hooks`` merges hook\n14\t   registrations *into* ``settings.json`` but never copies the source\n15\t   ``hooks.json`` to the target HOME. The acceptance criteria for T02.14\n16\t   require ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks.json`` to be byte-identical to\n17\t   ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` so post-mortem inspection and the\n18\t   FR-G* gate tests can SHA256-compare the source-of-truth registrations\n19\t   against what the per-eval HOME shipped. The adapter performs this\n20\t   second copy itself.\n21\t\n22\tDefense-in-depth: a pre-flight refusal asserts ``home_path`` is not the\n23\treal ``~/.claude/`` (resolves both sides; rejects any equality or\n24\tdescendant relationship). This complements but does not replace the\n25\t:class:`HomeIsolation` containment guard — the adapter MAY be invoked\n26\tdirectly by tests, so the guard must live here too.\n27\t\n28\tIdempotency: re-invoking ``deploy_hooks_to`` against the same\n29\t``home_path`` produces identical filesystem state. ``install_hooks``\n30\titself is idempotent (existing scripts are skipped without ``--force``;\n31\tthe settings.json merge skips matcher collisions). The verbatim\n32\t``hooks.json`` copy uses ``shutil.copy2`` which overwrites with the same\n33\tbytes when the source is unchanged.\n34\t\n35\tError contract: any failure inside the adapter raises\n36\t:class:`HookDeployFailed` with an :attr:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\n37\tattribute the EvalRunner (T03.x) propagates into\n38\t``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts``. The tag is a short kebab-case\n39\tidentifier the orchestrator buckets ERRORED evals by without parsing\n40\thuman-readable strings.\n41\t\n42\tCross-links\n43\t===========\n44\t\n45\t* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n46\t  ``home_path`` argument.\n47\t* NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13 / D-0033) — invokes the\n48\t  adapter from inside ``HomeIsolation.setup``'s post-mkdtemp block in\n49\t  a future wiring task.\n50\t* :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks.install_hooks` — the underlying\n51\t  installer this adapter wraps.\n52\t\"\"\"\n53\t\n54\tfrom __future__ import annotations\n55\t\n56\timport shutil\n57\tfrom pathlib import Path\n58\t\n59\tfrom ..install_hooks import install_hooks\n60\t\n61\t__all__ = [\n62\t    \"HookDeployFailed\",\n63\t    \"deploy_hooks_to\",\n64\t]\n65\t\n66\t\n67\t# Relative paths under the per-eval HOME that the adapter writes to.\n68\t_SETTINGS_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"settings.json\")\n69\t_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"hooks.json\")\n70\t\n71\t\n72\tclass HookDeployFailed(Exception):\n73\t    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`deploy_hooks_to` when hook deployment cannot complete.\n74\t\n75\t    Carries a short kebab-case :attr:`error_tag` the orchestrator\n76\t    (:class:`EvalRunner`, T03.x) routes into\n77\t    ``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`` so failed evals can be bucketed\n78\t    without parsing the human-readable message.\n79\t\n80\t    Attributes:\n81\t        error_tag: Short identifier for the failure mode. One of:\n82\t\n83\t            * ``\"refused-real-home\"`` — ``home_path`` resolved to (or\n84\t              under) the real ``~/.claude/``; defense-in-depth guard\n85\t              fired BEFORE any filesystem write.\n86\t            * ``\"install-hooks-failed\"`` — the underlying\n87\t              :func:`install_hooks` returned ``success=False``. The\n88\t              installer's message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n89\t            * ``\"hooks-json-copy-failed\"`` — the verbatim\n90\t              ``hooks.json`` copy raised :class:`OSError`. The OS\n91\t              error message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n92\t            * ``\"source-hooks-json-missing\"`` — the source\n93\t              ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` is not on disk.\n94\t              Should never happen in a working checkout; surfaced\n95\t              explicitly to make missing-source bugs loud.\n96\t\n97\t        home_path: The per-eval HOME the adapter was asked to deploy to.\n98\t        detail: Human-readable failure detail (installer message,\n99\t            ``OSError`` rendering, etc.). Never contains the error_tag\n100\t            itself so the two surfaces stay independent.\n101\t    \"\"\"\n102\t\n103\t    def __init__(\n104\t        self,\n105\t        *,\n106\t        error_tag: str,\n107\t        home_path: Path,\n108\t        detail: str,\n109\t    ) -> None:\n110\t        self.error_tag = error_tag\n111\t        self.home_path = home_path\n112\t        self.detail = detail\n113\t        super().__init__(\n114\t            f\"COMP-014 hook deploy failed [{error_tag}] for home_path={home_path!s}: {detail}\"\n115\t        )\n116\t\n117\t\n118\tdef _real_user_claude_dir() -> Path:\n119\t    \"\"\"Resolve the user's real ``~/.claude/`` directory.\n120\t\n121\t    Returns the resolved (symlinks collapsed) path. Used by the\n122\t    defense-in-depth refusal so a per-eval ``home_path`` that is a\n123\t    symlink to the real user HOME still fails the equality / descendant\n124\t    check.\n125\t    \"\"\"\n126\t\n127\t    return (Path.home() / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n128\t\n129\t\n130\tdef _refuse_real_home(home_path: Path) -> None:\n131\t    \"\"\"Reject ``home_path`` when any adapter write would land under real ``~/.claude/``.\n132\t\n133\t    Two attack surfaces are caught:\n134\t\n135\t    * ``home_path`` itself resolves to (or under) the real\n136\t      ``~/.claude/`` directory — every adapter write is anchored at\n137\t      ``home_path``, so this catches direct attempts.\n138\t    * ``home_path / .claude`` resolves to (or under) the real\n139\t      ``~/.claude/`` — the subtle case where the caller passes their\n140\t      own ``$HOME`` as ``home_path`` and the adapter's\n141\t      ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json`` write would otherwise\n142\t      silently target the real configuration directory.\n143\t\n144\t    Both sides are resolved with ``strict=False`` so a symlink in either\n145\t    direction is collapsed before comparison.\n146\t    \"\"\"\n147\t\n148\t    real = _real_user_claude_dir()\n149\t    resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=False)\n150\t    resolved_claude_subdir = (home_path / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n151\t\n152\t    def _matches(candidate: Path) -> bool:\n153\t        return candidate == real or candidate.is_relative_to(real)\n154\t\n155\t    if _matches(resolved_home) or _matches(resolved_claude_subdir):\n156\t        raise HookDeployFailed(\n157\t            error_tag=\"refused-real-home\",\n158\t            home_path=home_path,\n159\t            detail=(\n160\t                f\"home_path {home_path!s} (resolved={resolved_home!s}; \"\n161\t                f\"claude_subdir={resolved_claude_subdir!s}) intersects the \"\n162\t                f\"real user Claude dir {real!s}; refusing to write hooks \"\n163\t                f\"under the real ~/.claude/\"\n164\t            ),\n165\t        )\n166\t\n167\t\n168\tdef _source_hooks_json() -> Path:\n169\t    \"\"\"Locate the canonical ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json``.\n170\t\n171\t    Mirrors :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks._get_hooks_source` so\n172\t    the adapter and the installer agree on which file is the source of\n173\t    truth. Imported via the public ``install_hooks`` to avoid coupling\n174\t    on a private helper that may move.\n175\t    \"\"\"\n176\t\n177\t    # ``hook_adapter.py`` lives at src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py.\n178\t    # Two parents up is the package root (src/superclaude/).\n179\t    package_root = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent.parent\n180\t    return package_root / \"hooks\" / \"hooks.json\"\n181\t\n182\t\n183\tdef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None:\n184\t    \"\"\"Deploy SuperClaude hooks into the per-eval HOME at ``home_path``.\n185\t\n186\t    Two filesystem effects, in this order:\n187\t\n188\t    1. :func:`install_hooks` is invoked with ``target_path`` resolved to\n189\t       ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json``. This copies the hook\n190\t       scripts to ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks/`` and merges hook\n191\t       registrations into a fresh ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json``.\n192\t    2. The source ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` is copied\n193\t       verbatim to ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks.json`` (byte-identical\n194\t       via :func:`shutil.copy2`).\n195\t\n196\t    Idempotent: re-invocation on the same ``home_path`` produces\n197\t    identical filesystem state. The installer skips already-deployed\n198\t    scripts and matcher-colliding registrations; the verbatim copy\n199\t    overwrites with the same bytes when the source is unchanged.\n200\t\n201\t    Args:\n202\t        home_path: Per-eval HOME directory created by\n203\t            :class:`HomeIsolation.setup`. A separate copy\n   pipeline would drift; the COMP-014 reuse contract pins this to the\n   one installer.\n2. **Deploy ``hooks.json`` verbatim.** ``install_hooks`` merges hook\n   registrations *into* ``settings.json`` but never copies the source\n   ``hooks.json`` to the target HOME. The acceptance criteria for T02.14\n   require ``<home_path>/.claude/hooks.json`` to be byte-identical to\n   ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` so post-mortem inspection and the\n   FR-G* gate tests can SHA256-compare the source-of-truth registrations\n   against what the per-eval HOME shipped. The adapter performs this\n   second copy itself.\n\nDefense-in-depth: a pre-flight refusal asserts ``home_path`` is not the\nreal ``~/.claude/`` (resolves both sides; rejects any equality or\ndescendant relationship). This complements but does not replace the\n:class:`HomeIsolation` containment guard — the adapter MAY be invoked\ndirectly by tests, so the guard must live here too.\n\nIdempotency: re-invoking ``deploy_hooks_to`` against the same\n``home_path`` produces identical filesystem state. ``install_hooks``\nitself is idempotent (existing scripts are skipped without ``--force``;\nthe settings.json merge skips matcher collisions). The verbatim\n``hooks.json`` copy uses ``shutil.copy2`` which overwrites with the same\nbytes when the source is unchanged.\n\nError contract: any failure inside the adapter raises\n:class:`HookDeployFailed` with an :attr:`HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\nattribute the EvalRunner (T03.x) propagates into\n``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts``. The tag is a short kebab-case\nidentifier the orchestrator buckets ERRORED evals by without parsing\nhuman-readable strings.\n\nCross-links\n===========\n\n* COMP-006 :class:`HomeIsolation` (T02.11 / D-0032) — supplies the\n  ``home_path`` argument.\n* NFR-ISO2 atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13 / D-0033) — invokes the\n  adapter from inside ``HomeIsolation.setup``'s post-mkdtemp block in\n  a future wiring task.\n* :func:`superclaude.cli.install_hooks.install_hooks` — the underlying\n  installer this adapter wraps.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport shutil\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom ..install_hooks import install_hooks\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"HookDeployFailed\",\n    \"deploy_hooks_to\",\n]\n\n\n# Relative paths under the per-eval HOME that the adapter writes to.\n_SETTINGS_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"settings.json\")\n_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH = (\".claude\", \"hooks.json\")\n\n\nclass HookDeployFailed(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Raised by :func:`deploy_hooks_to` when hook deployment cannot complete.\n\n    Carries a short kebab-case :attr:`error_tag` the orchestrator\n    (:class:`EvalRunner`, T03.x) routes into\n    ``EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`` so failed evals can be bucketed\n    without parsing the human-readable message.\n\n    Attributes:\n        error_tag: Short identifier for the failure mode. The\n              installer's message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"hooks-json-copy-failed\"`` — the verbatim\n              ``hooks.json`` copy raised :class:`OSError`. The OS\n              error message is preserved in :attr:`detail`.\n            * ``\"source-hooks-json-missing\"`` — the source\n              ``src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json`` is not on disk.\n              Should never happen in a working checkout; surfaced\n              explicitly to make missing-source bugs loud.\n\n        home_path: The per-eval HOME the adapter was asked to deploy to.\n        detail: Human-readable failure detail (installer message,\n            ``OSError`` rendering, etc.). Never contains the error_tag\n            itself so the two surfaces stay independent.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        error_tag: str,\n        home_path: Path,\n        detail: str,\n    ) -> None:\n        self.error_tag = error_tag\n        self.home_path = home_path\n        self.detail = detail\n        super().__init__(\n            f\"COMP-014 hook deploy failed [{error_tag}] for home_path={home_path!s}: {detail}\"\n        )\n\n\ndef _real_user_claude_dir() -> Path:\n    \"\"\"Resolve the user's real ``~/.claude/`` directory.\n\n    Returns the resolved (symlinks collapsed) path. Used by the\n    defense-in-depth refusal so a per-eval ``home_path`` that is a\n    symlink to the real user HOME still fails the equality / descendant\n    check.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    return (Path.home() / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n\n\ndef _refuse_real_home(home_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Reject ``home_path`` when any adapter write would land under real ``~/.claude/``.\n\n    Two attack surfaces are caught:\n\n    * ``home_path`` itself resolves to (or under) the real\n      ``~/.claude/`` directory — every adapter write is anchored at\n      ``home_path``, so this catches direct attempts.\n    * ``home_path / .claude`` resolves to (or under) the real\n      ``~/.claude/`` — the subtle case where the caller passes their\n      own ``$HOME`` as ``home_path`` and the adapter's\n      ``<home_path>/.claude/settings.json`` write would otherwise\n      silently target the real configuration directory.\n\n    Both sides are resolved with ``strict=False`` so a symlink in either\n    direction is collapsed before comparison.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    real = _real_user_claude_dir()\n    resolved_home = home_path.resolve(strict=False)\n    resolved_claude_subdir = (home_path / \".claude\").resolve(strict=False)\n\n    def _matches(candidate: Path) -> bool:\n        return candidate == real or candidate.is_relative_to(real)\n\n    if _matches(resolved_home) or _matches(resolved_claude_subdir):\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"refused-real-home\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=(\n                f\"home_path {home_path!s} (resolved={resolved_home!s}; Required by the AC: the\n    # per-eval HOME must carry a SHA256-identical copy of the source\n    # registrations so post-mortem inspection / FR-G* gate tests can\n    # diff against the canonical file. Routed through the private\n    # :func:`_copy_hooks_json_verbatim` helper so test patches against\n    # ``shutil.copy2`` (a globally shared module attribute) cannot\n    # accidentally also break ``install_hooks``'s own copy pipeline.\n    dest_hooks_json = home_path.joinpath(*_HOOKS_JSON_RELPATH)\n    try:\n        dest_hooks_json.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n        _copy_hooks_json_verbatim(source_hooks_json, dest_hooks_json)\n    except OSError as exc:\n        raise HookDeployFailed(\n            error_tag=\"hooks-json-copy-failed\",\n            home_path=home_path,\n            detail=f\"verbatim hooks.json copy from {source_hooks_json!s} \"\n            f\"to {dest_hooks_json!s} failed: {exc}\",\n        ) from exc\n\n\ndef _copy_hooks_json_verbatim(src: Path, dest: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Copy ``src`` to ``dest`` verbatim (byte-identical, preserving mtime).\n\n    Wraps :func:`shutil.copy2` so the adapter's verbatim copy site is\n    a dedicated, monkeypatch-friendly seam. Pin a\n16\tsingle integration site at\n17\t`src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py` so the cliEval harness and\n18\tthe production user-install path stay byte-for-byte aligned.\n19\t\n20\t## Adapter contract\n21\t\n22\t### Signature\n23\t\n24\t```python\n25\tdef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None: ...\n26\t```\n27\t\n28\t`home_path` is the per-eval HOME directory `HomeIsolation.setup`\n29\tmaterialized (i.e., `<scratch_root>/<eval_id>-XXXXXX/`).
